## MJ-12 / Operation Majestic-12 Documents

MJ-12 / Operation Majestic-12 Documents is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1984 to 1990; axes C1 A1 D2; 6 connections; in the custody band.

In one line: Purported 1952 Eisenhower briefing document and associated papers, surfaced 1984-1987.

### What it is

A corpus of purported briefing papers that entered the public record as a roll of undeveloped 35mm film, postmarked Albuquerque, delivered anonymously to the Los Angeles home of television producer Jaime Shandera on 11 December 1984. Developed, the film produced an eight-page 'Briefing Document: Operation Majestic 12' dated 18 November 1952 and addressed to President-elect Eisenhower, naming twelve officials and describing the recovery of a crashed disc and four bodies near Roswell. Shandera, William L. Moore and Stanton Friedman released it publicly in May 1987. The only associated paper ever located inside a federal record group is the Cutler-Twining memorandum of 14 July 1954, found in Record Group 341.

### Why it is implicated

The corpus is on this board as a forgery with a provenance, not as evidence of a 1947 committee. It matters because the same city, the same interval and the same handful of intermediaries produced the Bennewitz material and the AQUARIUS paper, and because a document with the physical characteristics of a plant was found sitting inside a National Archives record group. A fabricated briefing paper is a curiosity. A fabricated briefing paper that arrives through an Air Force Office of Special Investigations channel and turns up filed in a federal record series is an operation, and the question the board keeps open is who ran it.

### The longer account

Every institutional body that has examined this corpus has rejected it, and the rejections are unusually specific. The National Archives searched Air Force, Joint Chiefs, National Security Council, Truman Library and Eisenhower Library holdings and came back with one item: the Cutler-Twining memorandum. NARA then enumerated what was wrong with it. There is no Top Secret register number. It sits in a folder containing no other MJ-12 material. It has no official letterhead and no watermark. Its 'Top Secret Restricted Information' marking is one the NSC's own FOIA office says did not come into use until the Nixon administration, which is to say two presidencies after the date on the paper. NARA's conservation specialist determined the sheet was a ribbon copy prepared on diction onionskin. The FBI's own account is blunter still: an Air Force investigation determined the document to be a fake.

The board's position is therefore not that the documents are disputed but that they are the wrong kind of object to dispute. They are dated 1952 and they behave like an artefact of 1984. The film arrived postmarked Albuquerque in December 1984; the AQUARIUS paper had been shown at Kirtland in April 1983; Moore has admitted cooperating with AFOSI; Richard Doty served as the conduit in the parallel Bennewitz and Howe channels. That is one city, one twenty-month window and one set of people. The board grades the inference Tier 3 and states its reasoning rather than its conclusion, because a concentration in place and time is a pattern and not a proof.

The most consequential fact about the corpus on this board is a silence. AARO's Historical Record Report of March 2024, tasked with resolving exactly this kind of allegation, does not address MJ-12 or AQUARIUS at all. Two of the most persistent alleged programme names in the entire historical record are absent from the report written to settle the historical record. Whatever the documents are, their omission is a fact about the report rather than about them.

### What it has denied

This is one of the few nodes on the board carrying a denial that is a directed act by a named denier. AARO's Historical Record Report is recorded as the denier, and the form the denial takes is omission: the report tasked with resolving the historical record does not address the corpus at all. Alongside that sits the FBI's account of an Air Force investigation that determined the document to be a fake, and NARA's itemised findings against the Cutler-Twining memorandum. The board's reading is that the paper is a forgery and the omission is still worth recording, because those are separate facts about separate institutions.

Roles the board assigns it: concealment, custody.

### Open questions on this node

- Nobody has ever identified who sent the film. The postmark is the only physical lead in the record and it establishes a city, not a sender.
- The eight-page briefing document itself has not been opened as a primary artefact for this board. What the board holds is the FBI's file, NARA's findings on the Cutler-Twining memorandum and the secondary literature; the corpus is characterised through its examiners rather than read directly.
- How a document with the physical characteristics NARA described came to be inside Record Group 341 is unexplained by any record opened here. NARA established what the paper is; no opened record establishes how it got into the file.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 277 of 1125, score 40). 6 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A1/D2.

- **Tier 1 documented.** NARA found that the Cutler-Twining memorandum bears no Top Secret register number, is filed in a folder containing no other MJ-12 material, lacks official letterhead or watermark, and carries a 'Top Secret Restricted Information' marking that the NSC FOIA office says did not come into use until the Nixon administration. # 2000-2016: AAWSAP / AATIP

## USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004

USS Nimitz Carrier Strike Group Encounters, 10-16 November 2004 is a event on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2004-11-10 to 2004-11-16; axes C1 A1 D3; 20 connections; in the custody band.

In one line: Multi-platform radar/visual/infrared UAP encounters, Southern California Operating Area; the 'Tic Tac'.

### What it is

On 14 November 2004, during pre-deployment workups off southern California, the USS Princeton (CG-59) vectored two F/A-18F Super Hornets from VFA-41 aboard USS Nimitz (CVN-68) onto a radar contact. Four aircrew, including squadron commanding officer Cdr. David Fravor, visually observed a white, wingless, roughly forty-foot object above a patch of disturbed water. A later sortie captured the infrared video now known as FLIR1, which the Department of Defense formally released as an authentic unclassified Navy video on 27 April 2020.

### Why it is implicated

Nimitz is the modern case that cannot be argued away on witness quality, because the observation was multi-platform: an Aegis SPY-1 radar track held over weeks, four aircrew in two aircraft, and an ATFLIR recording. What happened to that data afterwards is the point. Fravor testified under oath in July 2023 that "the incident was never investigated, none of my crew were ever questioned, tapes were never taken," and that no one from the government contacted him about it until Jay Stratton did so in 2009 on behalf of a Defense Department programme he had never heard of. Enlisted personnel from the Princeton and the Nimitz have separately said that unidentified individuals arrived by helicopter within a day and collected Aegis and aircraft data recordings; that account is testimonial and undocumented. Either way, the United States possessed the highest-quality sensor capture of an unexplained object in its history and produced no investigation, no incident report and no data package - until a compartmented programme quietly reached out five years later. That gap between the sensor and the file is the same architecture Bolender described in 1969.

### The longer account

The Nimitz encounter is the case that made the modern era possible, and the reason it belongs on a map of the legacy programme is not the object. It is the handling.

Fravor's sworn statement to the House Oversight Committee, filed for the hearing of 26 July 2023, is the cleanest primary account and it is worth reading for the operational detail rather than the spectacle. He was commanding officer of Strike Fighter Squadron 41 attached to Carrier Air Wing Eleven, roughly two weeks into an at-sea period, briefed to lead a two-versus-two air combat training exercise controlled by Princeton. On launch, "we were told that training was going to be suspended for real world tasking." His flight was vectored west of the combat air patrol point. Critically, he records what the controller told him about the preceding fortnight: the Princeton "had been observing these objects on their Aegis combat system for the previous 2 weeks. They had been descending from above 80,000ft and coming rapidly down to 20,000ft would stay for hours and then go straight back up." This is not a single sighting. It is a two-week radar campaign by a cruiser's air defence system that generated no public record and no known written report.

At the merge the aircrew saw disturbed white water, then a small white object with its longitudinal axis pointed north-south, moving abruptly above it, with "no Rotors, No Rotor wash, or any visible flight control surfaces like wings." Fravor descended in a clockwise turn; the object shifted its axis to align with his aircraft and began a climbing turn in the same direction. At roughly half a mile it "rapidly accelerated and disappeared right in front of our aircraft," and the disturbed water was gone with it. The flight turned back toward the CAP point sixty miles east and the controller reported that the object had reappeared there on the Princeton's SPY-1 radar, having covered that distance in "less than a minute."

Back aboard Nimitz they mentioned it to the crew preparing to launch. That crew took the roughly ninety-second infrared video that would be released by the government in 2017. Fravor adds a detail about a second recording that has never surfaced: "What is not seen is the Radar tape that showed the jamming of the APG-73 radar in the aircraft." An electronic attack on a fighter's fire control radar off the coast of California is, by any ordinary standard, a reportable event.

And then, on his account, nothing. "What is shocking is that the incident was never investigated, none of my crew were ever questioned, tapes were never taken, and after a couple of days, it turned into a great story to tell friends. Not until 2009 did Jay Stratton contact me to investigate what we observed. Unbeknownst to all of us, Jay was part of the ATIP . . . program led by Lue Elizondo out of the DOD." That single paragraph is the whole argument in miniature. The Navy's normal machinery - safety reporting, intelligence debrief, mishap investigation - did not engage. Five years later a compartmented Defense Department effort did, without announcing itself, and the resulting document circulated for years as what Fravor calls "the Unofficial Official Report." Fravor also notes that Lt. Cdr. Alex Dietrich, the other pilot in the flight, had been invited to the Pentagon multiple times to discuss the event and view other UAP videos while he had heard from no one at all - which means the government knew where the aircrew were and chose which of them to talk to.

Against that, there is a competing testimonial strand that must be tiered honestly. Petty Officer Gary Voorhis, aviation technician Patrick "PJ" Hughes and Senior Chief Kevin Day have described, in interviews and documentaries from roughly 2019 onward, unidentified individuals arriving by helicopter within about a day and taking Aegis data recordings, combat engagement centre recorders, optical drives holding radio communications and E-2 Hawkeye data bricks, with Voorhis instructed to erase even blank tapes. No document has ever been produced for this and no official Navy statement has addressed it. It is also not in tension with Fravor as often assumed: Fravor speaks for his aircrew and his squadron's tapes, the enlisted witnesses speak about the cruiser and the carrier's data systems. Both can be true. Neither is documented.

What is documented is the government's slow, deliberate concession of the artifact. On 27 April 2020 the Department of Defense issued a formal statement authorising release of three unclassified Navy videos, "one taken in November 2004 and the other two in January 2015, which have been circulating in the public domain after unauthorized releases in 2007 and 2017." It confirmed that "the U.S. Navy previously acknowledged that these videos circulating in the public domain were indeed Navy videos," stated that release "does not reveal any sensitive capabilities or systems, and does not impinge on any subsequent investigations of military air space incursions by unidentified aerial phenomena," and closed with the line that matters: "The aerial phenomena observed in the videos remain characterized as 'unidentified.'" Two things are conceded in that paragraph. The video is real, and there are subsequent investigations of airspace incursions by unidentified phenomena that a release could in principle impinge upon.

Fravor's closing testimony frames the oversight question the way this board frames it. He is explicit that the issue "is not about full public disclosure that could undermine national security" but about checks and balances, and he makes the structural point directly: "even Unacknowledged Waived programs have some level of oversight by the appropriate committee members in the House and Senate and this work that is said to be occurring from Whistleblower testimonies should not be exempt." That is a retired commander with a Top Gun background telling Congress, under oath, that he believes there is an activity operating outside the waived-programme oversight floor. Nimitz is on this map because it is the point where the sensor data, the missing file and the oversight gap all converge in one afternoon off San Diego.

### What it has denied

There is effectively no denial to catalogue, and that is itself the finding. The Navy acknowledged the videos' authenticity in 2019 and the Department of Defense formally released them on 27 April 2020 while stating that the phenomena "remain characterized as 'unidentified.'" No component has denied the Princeton's Aegis tracks, denied the encounter, or produced a resolution. The only official position that has ever been asserted about Nimitz is that the imagery is real and the object is unexplained.

Roles the board assigns it: concealment, disclosure, oversight, custody.

### Open questions on this node

- Do the USS Princeton's Aegis SPY-1 recordings and combat information centre logs for 10-16 November 2004 still exist, and under what disposition authority were they retained or destroyed?
- Does the APG-73 radar tape Fravor describes, showing jamming of his aircraft's fire control radar, exist in any Navy holding?
- Was any contemporaneous 2004 report of any kind generated - safety, intelligence, mishap or operational - and if not, under what authority was reporting waived for a two-week Aegis track and a fighter radar jamming event?
- What is the provenance, classification history and custodian of the document Fravor calls the "Unofficial Official Report," prepared following Jay Stratton's 2009 contact?
- Who directed the collection of data recordings from Princeton and Nimitz, if it occurred, and under what authority? No FOIA release has yet produced tasking, travel or custody documentation.
- Why was Lt. Cdr. Dietrich brought to the Pentagon repeatedly while the flight lead was not contacted at all, and which office issued those invitations?

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 94 of 1125, score 64). 21 connections, 5 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D3.

## Disclosure Project Briefing Document, April 2001

Disclosure Project Briefing Document, April 2001 is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2001-04; axes C0 A0 D3; 10 connections; in the custody band.

In one line: Roughly 500 page compilation prepared by Steven M. Greer MD and Theodore C. Loder III PhD, printing summarised witness testimony.

### What it is

The Disclosure Project Briefing Document of April 2001, roughly 500 pages, written and compiled by Steven M. Greer MD and Theodore C. Loder III PhD and addressed on its own title page to members of the press, of the United States government and of the US scientific community. Its section 3.8 prints sixty-eight dated witness testimonies across five subsections, sixty-five of them dated between July 2000 and early 2001. The interviews are conducted by Greer himself, each carrying an initials key of the form 'JC: FAA Division Chief John Callahan SG: Dr. Steven Greer', with his editorial comments printed inline.

### Why it is implicated

It is the single openable anchor under an entire generation of claims. A large share of what circulates about retrieval, custody and reverse engineering traces to a named testimony in this document and to nothing earlier, which makes it the most efficient node on this board: one artefact, sixty-eight dated statements, all readable. It is on the map because it is the origin point in print for more of this subject than any other single object the board carries.

### The longer account

This document is the reason the Disclosure Project cluster could be built cheaply and graded honestly at the same time. Sixty-eight testimonies, each under a name, a rank, a service and a month, is a rare thing in this subject: most claims arrive undated and unattributed, and these do not.

What the document is NOT is evidence for what the testimonies describe, and the board's governing rule for this whole cluster is written as a Tier 3 claim on this node so that nobody has to infer it. The document is Tier 1 for the fact that a named person gave testimony on a stated date. It is Tier 2 at best for anything that testimony asserts about the world. Every witness node built from it carries that distinction in its own claims.

Two features of the document itself deserve a reader's attention. The compiler cross-references his own witnesses to each other in bracketed editorial notes signed 'SG', which means the apparent convergence between two testimonies can be the editor's construction rather than the witnesses'. And the interviews are all conducted by one man, so the sixty-eight are not sixty-eight independent methods. That is the same caution the board applies to the Castello fabrication in the Dulce cluster, arrived at from the opposite direction.

The research pass of 19 September also found what the document does not support. It prints McCandlish's testimony dated December 2000, five months before the press conference that is routinely said to be where he spoke. Of sixty-nine interviewees, twenty spoke at that event, and only five can be placed at the podium by any opened source. The document and the press conference are different objects and the board now carries them as different nodes.

Roles the board assigns it: disclosure, custody, research.

### Open questions on this node

- The full witness list against the podium roster. Of sixty-nine interviewees, twenty spoke at the 9 May 2001 press conference and only five are placed there by any opened source. The other fifteen are unidentified.
- Whether any testimony in the document was recorded other than by Greer himself. Nothing opened indicates so.
- The video recordings the document summarises. None was opened; the board carries the printed summaries only.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 200 of 1125, score 45). 12 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D3.

## Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection

Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection is a institution on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2009; axes C1 A0 D3; 8 connections; in the custody band.

In one line: Arquivo Nacional (Brazil), Fundo Objeto Voador Não Identificado – OVNI, reference BR DFANBSB ARX, 743 registros, produced by the Comando da Aeronáutica.

### What it is

The Fundo Objeto Voador Nao Identificado (OVNI), reference code BR DFANBSB ARX, is a discrete archival collection held by Brazil's Arquivo Nacional, the national archives, and produced by the Comando da Aeronautica. It contains reports, occurrence questionnaires, correspondence, photographs, drawings, video, audio and press clippings concerning objects observed in Brazilian skies. It is open to the public and searchable online through SIAN, the Arquivo Nacional's information system.

### Why it is implicated

This is the physical answer to the question the US disclosure fight keeps asking in the abstract: what does it actually look like when a state moves its military UAP holdings into civilian custody and publishes them. It looks like a reference code, a finding aid, a login page and a holding count. The archive is the endpoint of the pipeline the FAB built through Portaria 551/GC3, and its existence dismantles the premise that this material is intrinsically unreleasable, because a NATO-adjacent air force with a real air defence mission released it and continues to accession into it annually. It is also honest about its own limits, and the board should be too: the collection covers one service, the Aeronautica, and not the Army or the Navy, and its holding count is reported differently by the archive and by the Senate. Those limits do not weaken the comparison. They sharpen it, because even a partial, imperfect, single-service release is more than the United States has produced in seventy-nine years.

### The longer account

Brazil's national archive holds the world's most accessible body of military records on unidentified aerial objects, and it holds them under an ordinary archival reference code with an ordinary finding aid, which is precisely what makes it remarkable. The Arquivo Nacional describes the collection plainly on its own website: the Fundo Objeto Voador Nao Identificado, BR DFANBSB ARX, produced by the Comando da Aeronautica do Brasil and held in the archive's custody, composed of 743 registros comprising reports, occurrence questionnaires, correspondence, photographs, drawings, videos, audio and clippings from newspapers and magazines about objects seen in Brazilian skies over the years. The earliest occurrence in the fund dates from 1952 and, per the archive's own description, the most recent from 2016. Access runs through SIAN, the Sistema de Informacoes do Arquivo Nacional, and a curated image set is published on the archive's Pinterest account, which is not a detail one expects from a body of military intelligence records.

The holding count is contested and the board should present both figures rather than pick one. The Arquivo Nacional says 743 registros. The Brazilian Senate's own record, in the justification to RQS 193/2022, says that searching the term OVNI alone returns 137 records but that entering the specific reference code BR DFANBSB ARX returns 758 documents. These are almost certainly counting different things, registros as archival units versus digitised items as retrieved by a code search, and the gap may also reflect accruals between the two dates. Neither figure is wrong and neither is authoritative for the other's purpose. Both are cited here. Layered on top, the ufologist A. J. Gevaerd told the Senate plenary in June 2022 that the Brazilian government and the Aeronautica had released more than 20,000 pages, which is a page count rather than an item count and is not in conflict with either.

The collection's provenance runs through the FAB's documentation and archival machinery rather than through litigation or leak. From 2007 the Aeronautica began sending its papers to the archive. In April 2009 CENDOC forwarded the first Operacao Prato material. In August 2010 Portaria no 551/GC3 formalised the flow, making the archive the default destination for reports received through the aerospace operations command. The consequence is that the fund is not a historical dump frozen at a cut-off date but a live series. In August 2026 Brazilian media reporting off documents released through the archive identified at least eighteen unidentified-object cases reported in Brazil during 2025 by pilots, controllers and other aviation personnel, with five clustered at Londrina in Parana and four in Ceara, including a LATAM captain's report near Guarulhos on 1 September 2025 and a Londrina tower controller's 23 November 2025 report of a white, round object that appeared and disappeared on the traffic display. A citizen can read the form.

The archive is not an advocate, and its framing is worth cataloguing precisely because it is not. Its public page carries an explicit caveat that OVNI does not necessarily mean flying saucers or extraterrestrials, and that the term designates any flying object whose origin was not immediately identified, which may include satellites, drones, balloons or natural phenomena. On this board that sentence is an artefact rather than a rebuttal: it is the institutional hedge a records body attaches when it publishes material a defence ministry produced, and its presence tells you the archive expected the material to be read as more than administrative residue. The important thing is that the hedge is attached to published records rather than used as a reason not to publish.

The boundary of the collection is the sharpest single fact in this dossier. The Senate record states without qualification that from 2007 it was the Aeronautica, and not the Exercito and not the Marinha, that sent its papers. Brazilian researchers went to the Ministry of Defence in April 2013, at the invitation of then Foreign Minister Celso Amorim and before the service commanders, specifically to press for the Army and Navy holdings. Those holdings have not been released. So the Fundo OVNI is a one-service archive, which means Brazil's disclosure is real but partial, and means the comparison with the United States should be drawn carefully: Brazil is not a state that opened everything, it is a state where one armed service unilaterally chose publication as its default and the other two did not. That variance is the most useful thing about it, because it isolates the decision. The material did not force the outcome. The institution chose it. And in the United States, no service has chosen it, at any point, in any decade, for any category of this material.

### What it has denied

The archive itself does not deny; it disclaims. Its public description states that OVNI does not necessarily mean flying saucers or extraterrestrials and that the label covers satellites, drones, balloons and natural phenomena. That is a records body inoculating itself against the reading its own holdings invite, and it is catalogued here as an institutional hedge attached to published material rather than as a reason for withholding it.

Roles the board assigns it: custody, disclosure, research.

### Open questions on this node

- The 743 versus 758 discrepancy has not been reconciled. The two figures come from different bodies, are counting differently (archival registros versus documents returned by a reference-code search), and are separated in time. No authoritative reconciliation exists in the sources located.
- The Arquivo Nacional gives 2016 as the fund's most recent occurrence while the FAB states transfers run through 2025 and 2025 reports are demonstrably being released. Whether the archive page is simply out of date, or whether post-2016 accruals sit under a separate series, is unresolved.
- The Army and Navy holdings referred to in the Senate record and sought at the April 2013 Ministry of Defence meeting have not been released and their extent is unknown.
- Whether any material was withheld, redacted or reclassified during the transfers from CENDOC and GSI is not addressed in any located source.
- Whether the fund contains any material relating to physical residue, recovered objects or materials analysis has not been established from the finding aid.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 68 of 1125, score 73). 10 connections, 6 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Accession-by-accession structure is documented: a 2011 parliamentary requirement refers to 'o terceiro recolhimento do CENDOC (FAB) ao Arquivo Nacional (COREG)', citing 'envelope nº 2 do terceiro recolhimento', referring to the year 1981; and records that the Minister of Defence had stated CENDOC 'encerraria no dia 31 de agosto de 2010 a remessa de todo o seu acervo à Coordenação Regional do Arquivo Nacional no Distrito Federal (COREG)'. Source: Câmara dos Deputados, RIC 679/2011, justificação and item 2.4, 22 June 2011, referencing RIC 4470/2009 answered by Minister Nelson Jobim via Ofício 4225-GM/Aspar-MD of 20 April 2010
- **Tier 1 documented.** CONTRADICTION RESOLVED - the 2016 date is a stale description, not a fund closing date. The Arquivo Nacional's own statement - 'Ele é composto de 743 registros' and 'A primeira ocorrência do Fundo Objeto Voador Não Identificado data de 1952 e a mais recente de 2016' - appears on an institutional news item carrying the byline 'Publicado em 13/09/2018 18h25 Atualizado em 13/09/2018 18h27'. It is a snapshot as of September 2018 that has never been revised, not a description of an accession that ended. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheça o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', published 13/09/2018 (https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional)
- **Tier 1 documented.** HOLDING-COUNT DISCREPANCY RESOLVED - 743 and 758 count different things at different dates by different methods. 743 is the Arquivo Nacional's own 2018 description of the fund's registros. 758 is a SIAN search-hit count reported in 2022: 'Atualmente, no Arquivo Nacional, quando se pesquisa apenas o termo OVNI, se encontram 137 registros, mas se colocarmos na pesquisa um código de referência específico (BR DFANBSB ARX), encontraremos 758 documentos.' The same sentence yields 137 for a free-text search, proving these are query results, not holdings inventories. Neither number is a stable figure for an accruing fund. Source: Senado Federal, RQS 193/2022, justificação; and Arquivo Nacional news item 13/09/2018
- **Tier 1 documented.** Individual records are served with full archival references and page images, e.g. BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249 for the 2 June 1986 Air Defence Command report. Source: Arquivo Nacional SIAN image server
- **Tier 1 documented.** Speaking in the Senate plenary on 24 June 2022, the ufologist and journalist A. J. Gevaerd stated that the Brazilian government and the Aeronautica had released more than 20,000 pages of documents now held at the Arquivo Nacional in Brasilia, and that the opening 'is not complete' but had a very fruitful beginning. Source: Agencia Senado, 'Em homenagem no Plenario, ufologos cobram liberacao de documentos sobre ovnis', 24 June 2022, https://www12.senado.leg.br/noticias/materias/2022/06/24/em-homenagem-no-plenario-ufologos-cobram-liberacao-de-documentos-sobre-ovnis
- **Tier 1 documented.** The 2016 endpoint was still being repeated by COMAER in 2022 litigation, showing the lag is institutional and not confined to the Arquivo Nacional: 'a coleção filmográfica, iconográfica, sonora e textual referente aos anos de 1952 e 2016, produzida pelo COMAER, está disponível para consulta no acervo do Arquivo Nacional.' Source: Justiça Federal SC, Recurso Cível n. 5002417-48.2022.4.04.7200/SC, RELVOTO 27/2/2023, quoting Comando de Operações Especiais/Comando da Aeronáutica, evento 13, ANEXO2
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Arquivo Nacional attaches an explicit caveat to its description of the fund, stating that OVNI does not necessarily signify the existence of flying saucers or extraterrestrials and that the term designates any flying object whose origin was not immediately identified, which may include satellites, drones, balloons or natural phenomena. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheca o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Arquivo Nacional holds the Fundo Objeto Voador Nao Identificado (OVNI) under reference code BR DFANBSB ARX, produced by the Comando da Aeronautica do Brasil. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheca o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Arquivo Nacional identifies the holding by name and reference: 'o Arquivo Nacional possui o Fundo Objeto Voador Não Identificado – OVNI (BR DFANBSB ARX)? O acervo sobre OVNIs é produzido pelo Comando da Aeronáutica do Brasil e está sob a guarda do Arquivo Nacional. Ele é composto de 743 registros que incluem relatos, questionários sobre ocorrências, correspondências, fotografias, desenhos, vídeos, áudios e recortes de jornais e revistas a respeito de objetos que foram vistos nos céus brasileiros ao longo dos anos.' Source: Arquivo Nacional (Brasil), 'Conheça o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional'
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Arquivo Nacional states that the earliest occurrence in the fund dates from 1952 and the most recent from 2016. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheca o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Arquivo Nacional states that the fund is composed of 743 registros, including reports, occurrence questionnaires, correspondence, photographs, drawings, videos, audio and newspaper and magazine clippings. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheca o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Brazilian Senate's Requerimento 193/2022 dates the transfer route to 2007 and states that the Aeronáutica alone transferred, not the Army or the Navy: 'A partir de 2007, a Aeronáutica, mas não o Exército e nem a Marinha, passou a enviar seus papeis resultantes de suas pesquisas para o Arquivo Nacional, em Brasília. E ano após ano, até o momento, novos documentos ufológicos tiveram o mesmo destino, somando-se hoje mais de 20 mil páginas de arquivos liberados.' It also records that in August 2010 Brigadeiro Junichi Saito, then FAB commander, directed by decree published in the Diário Oficial da União that all FAB UFO cases be concentrated at COMDABRA for shipment to the Arquivo Nacional. Source: Senado Federal, RQS 193/2022
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Brazilian Senate's record gives a different figure: searching the term OVNI alone in the Arquivo Nacional returns 137 registros, while searching the specific reference code BR DFANBSB ARX returns 758 documents. The 743 and 758 figures come from different bodies at different dates and neither has been reconciled to the other. Source: Senado Federal, Requerimento no 193, de 2022, justificacao, presented 16 March 2022, https://legis.senado.leg.br/sdleg-getter/documento?dm=9089831&disposition=inline
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Senate record states that from 2007 the Aeronautica, but not the Exercito and not the Marinha, began sending its papers to the Arquivo Nacional, and that the total released amounts to more than 20,000 pages. Source: Senado Federal, RQS 193/2022, justificacao, https://legis.senado.leg.br/sdleg-getter/documento?dm=9089831&disposition=inline
- **Tier 1 documented.** The fund is accessible to the public through the Sistema de Informacoes do Arquivo Nacional (SIAN) at http://sian.an.gov.br/sianex/consulta/login.asp, and a set of images from the collection is published on the Arquivo Nacional's Pinterest profile. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheca o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 1 documented.** The mechanism that makes the fund open-ended is written into the governing order: Portaria 551/GC3 Art. 3 requires CENDOC to 'copiar, encadernar, arquivar cópias dos registros encaminhados pelo COMDABRA e enviar, periodicamente, os originais ao Arquivo Nacional.' The fund therefore grows by periodic recolhimentos; a fixed terminal year is structurally impossible while the Portaria is in force. Source: Portaria nº 551/GC3, de 9 de agosto de 2010, Art. 3º, transcribed in Justiça Federal SC, Recurso Cível n. 5002417-48.2022.4.04.7200/SC, RELVOTO 27/2/2023
- **Tier 1 documented.** The producing body is registered in the Arquivo Nacional's authority-record system, Dibrarq, as Ministerio da Defesa (Brasil), Comando da Aeronautica. Source: Arquivo Nacional, Dibrarq authority record, https://dibrarq.arquivonacional.gov.br/index.php/ministerio-da-defesa-brasil-comando-da-aeronautica;isaar?sf_culture=pt_BR
- **Tier 1 documented.** Verified item-level reference code format, published by the Arquivo Nacional itself: 'Ilustração elaborada a partir de relatos de aparição de objeto voador não identificado em Marília, São Paulo, agosto de 1968. Arquivo Nacional. Fundo Objeto Voador Não Identificado. BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_25.' This confirms the BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_nnn item numbering used elsewhere on the board. Source: Arquivo Nacional, 'Conheça o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', 13/09/2018, image caption
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'Hoje, o acervo sobre óvnis é o segundo mais acessado do Arquivo Nacional - só perde para os relatórios da ditadura militar. O material abrange um período de 64 anos e vai de 1952 ... até 2016.' Source: BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 'Por meio de nota, a Aeronáutica informou que todo o material disponível sobre óvnis já foi encaminhado ao Arquivo Nacional. E mais: não dispõe de profissionais especializados para realizar investigações científicas ou emitir parecer a respeito deste tipo de fenômeno aéreo.' Source: BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** 2025 records are demonstrably in the fund and being served: the Arquivo Nacional released FAB dossiers covering at least 18 unidentified-object reports made in Brazil during 2025 by pilots and air traffic controllers, including a 23 November 2025 Londrina control-tower report and a 1 September 2025 report by a LATAM captain over Guarulhos (SP). State distribution reported: Paraná 5, Ceará 4, Minas Gerais 2, São Paulo 2, Rio de Janeiro 2, Alagoas 1, Mato Grosso 1, undetermined 1. Source: André Rigue and Lauryn Amaral, CNN Brasil, 'Pilotos e controladores relataram 18 casos de OVNIs no Brasil em 2025', 12 August 2026, updated 14 August 2026 (https://www.cnnbrasil.com.br/ciencia/pilotos-e-controladores-relataram-18-casos-de-ovnis-no-brasil-em-2025/)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** An earliest item in the collection is a record of a sighting at Barra da Tijuca, in the west zone of Rio de Janeiro, in 1952. Source: Correio Braziliense, 'Arquivo Nacional guarda ao menos 743 relatos de ovnis no Brasil', February 2023, https://www.correiobraziliense.com.br/brasil/2023/02/5073573-arquivo-nacional-guarda-ao-menos-743-relatos-de-ovnis-no-brasil.html
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On 10 August 2010 EFE reported 'FAB cria normas para pilotos em caso de contato com ovnis'; on 14 August 2010 O Estado de S. Paulo reported 'Aeronáutica libera documentos sobre aparição de óvnis'. Source: EFE/Terra, 10 August 2010; Pedro Dantas, O Estado de S. Paulo, 14 August 2010
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The FAB states that its holdings for the period 1952 to 2025 have been transferred, which extends nine years beyond the terminal date the Arquivo Nacional gives for the fund. Source: Nota da Forca Aerea reproduced in CNN Brasil, 12 August 2026, https://www.cnnbrasil.com.br/ciencia/pilotos-e-controladores-relataram-18-casos-de-ovnis-no-brasil-em-2025/ ; compare Arquivo Nacional, https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The Ministry of Justice and Public Security, the Arquivo Nacional's parent ministry, has publicly characterised the OVNI collection as among the most consulted holdings at the Arquivo Nacional. Source: Ministerio da Justica e Seguranca Publica, 'Acervo sobre OVNIs e um dos mais visitados no Arquivo Nacional', https://www.justica.gov.br/news/acervo-sobre-ovnis-e-um-dos-mais-visitados-no-arquivo-nacional

A further 2 claims sit on this node on the board and are not reproduced here.

### Its strongest connections

- Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection comes in from **Força Aérea Brasileira / Ministério da Aeronáutica** (institution, board tier A), a programmatic link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Portaria 551/GC3 (9 Aug 2010): COMDABRA/COMAE catalogues, CENDOC forwards originals periodically. Art. 3 mandates periodic transfer of originals, which is why the fund has no terminal year. Published DOU 10 Aug 2010, Seção 1, p. 101. Revoked Nota C-002/MIN/ADM (13 Apr 1978) and Aviso S-001/MIN (28 Feb 1989). Successive accessions are documented at least to a 'terceiro recolhimento' covering 1981, with CENDOC's bulk remittance to COREG scheduled to close 31 Aug 2010; FAB states coverage now runs 1952-2025. Sources: https://www.migalhas.com.br/arquivos/2026/1/7F5E4CC12D7AE7_terceiraacao.pdf, https://www.migalhas.com.br/arquivos/2026/1/7F5E4CC12D7AE7_terceiraacao.pdf; https://www.camara.leg.br/proposicoesWeb/prop_mostrarintegra?codteor=891762&filename=RIC+679%2F2011, https://www.camara.leg.br/proposicoesWeb/prop_mostrarintegra?codteor=891762&filename=RIC+679%2F2011; https://www.cnnbrasil.com.br/ciencia/pilotos-e-controladores-relataram-18-casos-de-ovnis-no-brasil-em-2025/, https://www.cnnbrasil.com.br/ciencia/pilotos-e-controladores-relataram-18-casos-de-ovnis-no-brasil-em-2025/; Relatório de Ocorrências (Arquivo Nacional), http://imagem.sian.an.gov.br/acervo/derivadas/BR_DFANBSB_ARX/0/0/0249/BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249_d0001de0001.pdf, dated 1986-06-02; BBC Brasil, https://www.bbc.com/portuguese/brasil-59953278, dated 2022-01-14.
- Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection runs out to **National Archives Record Group 615 - UAP Records Collection** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: COMPARISON: a voluntary military transfer into a civil archive versus a statutory collection ordered by Congress. COMPARISON EDGE, no institutional contact between the Arquivo Nacional and NARA is claimed or evidenced. Brazil: from 2007 the Comando da Aeronáutica began sending its holdings to the Arquivo Nacional of its own motion, producing the Fundo Objeto Voador Não Identificado – OVNI (BR DFANBSB ARX), 743 registros, served online, with the service publicly disclaiming further competence. The United States: the UAP Records Collection in Record Group 615 exists only because sections 1841–1843 of Public Law 118-31 ordered it into being, with a statutory transfer deadline and with the independent review board stripped out in conference. Same archival endpoint, opposite mechanism, one service handed its record over, the other had to be legislated at. [CORRECTED 14 Aug 2026: RQS 193/2022 is a request for a COMMEMORATIVE SPECIAL SESSION under Art. 199 RISF, approved 16 Mar 2022, held 24 Jun 2022 - not a records-seeking or compelling instrument. Its durable value is the content of its justificacao, which names Operacao Prato, the 1986 night, SIOANI and the archive reference BR DFANBSB ARX. These edges assert what the document NAMES, which is verifiable in its text and unaffected by its procedural nature, so the tier is unchanged. Any claim that the instrument COMPELLED disclosure would be false.] Sources: Arquivo Nacional (Brasil), 'Conheça o fundo sobre OVNIs do Arquivo Nacional', https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br/canais_atendimento/imprensa/noticias/conheca-o-fundo-sobre-ovnis-do-arquivo-nacional; Senado Federal (Brasil), Requerimento nº 193, de 2022 (RQS 193/2022), autoria do Senador Eduardo Girão (PODEMOS/CE) com Eliziane Gama, Alessandro Vieira, Izalci Lucas, Jorge Kajuru, Marcos do Val, Paulo Rocha e Reguffe, sessão especial para os 75 anos do Dia Mundial da Ufologia, https://legis.senado.leg.br/sdleg-getter/documento?dm=9089831&ts=1647523438292&disposition=inline; FY2024 National Defense Authorization Act, Public Law 118-31, sections 1841–1843 (Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Collection at NARA); Senado Federal, Dados Abertos, official record of RQS 193/2022 (Código 152222): ementa 'Sessão especial destinada a comemorar os 75 anos do Dia Mundial da Ufologia', 16 March 2022, Sen. Eduardo Girão and seven co-signers, https://legis.senado.leg.br/dadosabertos/materia/pesquisa/lista?sigla=RQS&numero=193&ano=2022, dated 2026-08-23; Relatório de Ocorrências (Arquivo Nacional PDF), http://imagem.sian.an.gov.br/acervo/derivadas/BR_DFANBSB_ARX/0/0/0249/BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249_d0001de0001.pdf, dated 1986-06-02; Arquivo Nacional - portal, https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br, dated 2026-08-12.
- Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection comes in from **Operação Prato (Operation Saucer)** (program, board tier A), a programmatic link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: SIAN search 'operação prato' returns 6 documents, none audiovisual. Arquivo Nacional's own answer to a litigant, quoted in the 2023 federal judgment. Contrasts with the 2011 parliamentary finding that hundreds of photographs (of 500+) and hours of super 8 / super 16mm film were never transferred from I COMAR. The court found insufficient evidence that the audiovisual files exist; the parliamentary record asserts they did. Both positions are on the record and are not reconciled. Sources: https://www.migalhas.com.br/arquivos/2026/1/7F5E4CC12D7AE7_terceiraacao.pdf, https://www.migalhas.com.br/arquivos/2026/1/7F5E4CC12D7AE7_terceiraacao.pdf; https://www.camara.leg.br/proposicoesWeb/prop_mostrarintegra?codteor=891762&filename=RIC+679%2F2011, https://www.camara.leg.br/proposicoesWeb/prop_mostrarintegra?codteor=891762&filename=RIC+679%2F2011; Operação Prato - Wikipedia, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera%C3%A7%C3%A3o_Prato, dated 2026-01-01; Arquivo Nacional - portal, https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br, dated 2026-08-12.
- Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection runs out to **Sky Canada Project - Office of the Chief Science Advisor of Canada** (program, board tier B), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: COMPARISON: Canada surveyed the world's UAP-reporting models and did not mention Brazil once. COMPARISON EDGE, and a second sourced negative. The Sky Canada Project report examined 'the management of UAP reporting in several countries' and concluded that 'the processes in place in a few countries, notably the United States, France and Chile, provide interesting examples of coordinated, transparent, and scientifically driven approaches'. Its recommendation 13 tells the future Canadian lead organisation to 'establish partnerships with international entities dedicated to UAPs, such as AARO and NASA (U.S.A.), GEIPAN (France), and SEFAA (Chile).' The word 'Brazil' does not occur in the report. The state that actually put its military UAP files in a public archive is absent from the survey of states that handle UAP reporting well, which is a finding about the survey as much as about Brazil. Sources: Office of the Chief Science Advisor of Canada, 'Management of Public Reporting of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in Canada' (Sky Canada Project, 2025), https://science.gc.ca/site/science/en/office-chief-science-advisor/sky-canada-project/management-public-reporting-unidentified-aerial-phenomena-canada; Arquivo Nacional - portal, https://www.gov.br/arquivonacional/pt-br, dated 2026-08-12.
- Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection comes in from **Senado Federal do Brasil** (institution, board tier C), a legislative link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: The Senate's own 2022 instrument counts and cites the archive. RQS 193/2022 recites the transfer route from 2007 onward, 'somando-se hoje mais de 20 mil páginas de arquivos liberados', names Brigadeiro Junichi Saito's August 2010 decree in the Diário Oficial da União directing that all FAB UFO cases be concentrated at COMDABRA for shipment to the Arquivo Nacional, and gives the archive's own search behaviour: 'quando se pesquisa apenas o termo OVNI, se encontram 137 registros, mas se colocarmos na pesquisa um código de referência específico (BR DFANBSB ARX), encontraremos 758 documentos.' A national legislature citing archival reference codes in a legislative instrument is the specific thing the US board has no equivalent of. Eduardo Girão and seven co-signers, https://legis.senado.leg.br/dadosabertos/materia/pesquisa/lista?sigla=RQS&numero=193&ano=2022, dated 2026-08-23; Relatório de Ocorrências (Arquivo Nacional PDF), http://imagem.sian.an.gov.br/acervo/derivadas/BR_DFANBSB_ARX/0/0/0249/BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249_d0001de0001.pdf, dated 1986-06-02; Senado Federal - Dados Abertos, list of senators in exercise (official open-data service, 81 members), https://legis.senado.leg.br/dadosabertos/senador/lista/atual, dated 2026-08-12.
- Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection comes in from **Brasilia, Federal District, Brazil** (location, board tier C), a custody link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: The UFO fund is held in Brasília and its reference code says so. DFANBSB is the archival code for the Arquivo Nacional's Brasília unit. - Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection comes in from **Força Aérea Brasileira / Ministério da Aeronáutica** (institution, board tier A), a custody link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: transferred its entire UAP holding and disclaimed competence. The Air Force told BBC Brasil that all available material has gone to the Arquivo Nacional and that it has no specialists able to investigate or opine on the phenomena. Sources: BBC Brasil, https://www.bbc.com/portuguese/brasil-59953278, dated 2022-01-14.
- Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection runs out to **GEIPAN - Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifiés** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 2 named person on the record. Brazil gives the citizen the primary document and no adjudication; France gives the citizen the adjudication. The board should not treat these as the same category of openness. ## USS Theodore Roosevelt Encounters (2014-2015)

USS Theodore Roosevelt Encounters (2014-2015) is a event on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2014 to 2015; axes C0 A1 D3; 8 connections; in the custody band.

In one line: Multi-aircrew UAP encounter series, Atlantic warning areas.

### What it is

The sustained series of unidentified object encounters reported by U.S. Navy aircrews operating with Carrier Air Wing One and the USS Theodore Roosevelt carrier strike group off the U.S. East Coast from roughly the summer of 2014 through the Roosevelt's 2015 deployment work-ups. The episode produced the two ATFLIR infrared videos known publicly as GIMBAL and GOFAST, both of which the Navy has officially dated to 21 January 2015.

### Why it is implicated

This is the case in which a U.S. Navy fighter squadron documented an unidentified object corridor over an active training range for months, and the institutional response was to erase the paperwork rather than identify the objects. Ryan Graves testified under oath that after a near-collision at the W-72 entry point his squadron submitted a safety report and there was "no official acknowledgement of the incident and no further mechanism to report the sightings." The stronger allegation, entered into the same congressional record, comes from Rear Admiral Tim Gallaudet: a 2015 SIPRNET email from the Fleet Forces operations commander to all one- and two-star admirals, titled "Urgent safety of flight issue" and attaching the GO FAST video, was removed from his and his deputy's classified systems the next day and never discussed, which he attributes to a classified special access program. That is a documented instance of a compartmented program reaching into a flag officer's classified mail to delete a safety-of-flight warning. Meanwhile the Navy's own on-record position is that it "considers the phenomena contained/depicted in those 3 videos as unidentified" and will provide "no details on individual reports."

### The longer account

In 2014, Lieutenant Ryan Graves was flying F/A-18F Super Hornets with Strike Fighter Squadron 11 out of the Virginia Beach area. Following an upgrade to the squadron's radar, aircrews began detecting unknown objects in their operating airspace. The tracks were initially dismissed as software glitches until they were corroborated with infrared sensors, at which point, in Graves's words, the sightings "became an open secret among our aircrew" and "were so frequent that they became part of daily briefs."

The pivotal incident occurred in Warning Area W-72, a block of restricted airspace ten miles east of Virginia Beach through which all traffic enters at a single GPS point and set altitude. Graves testified: "Just at the moment the two jets crossed the threshold, one of the pilots saw a dark gray cube inside of a clear sphere, motionless against the wind, fixed directly at the entry point. The jets, only 100 feet apart, were forced to take evasive action. They terminated the mission immediately and returned to base. Our squadron submitted a safety report, but there was no official acknowledgement of the incident and no further mechanism to report the sightings." His description of the objects' performance is specific and falsifiable: they "could accelerate at speeds up to Mach 1, hold their position against hurricane-force winds, and outlast our fighter jets, operating continuously throughout the day," with "no visible means of lift, control surfaces or propulsion."

The cover-up allegation is Gallaudet's, and Graves put it in the record. While serving as an admiral at Fleet Forces Command, Gallaudet received a classified SIPRNET email in 2015 from his boss, the Operations Commander, addressed to all one- and two-star admiral subordinates. The subject line was "Urgent safety of flight issue." Attached was the GO FAST video from a Navy F/A-18. The email asked whether anyone knew the objects' origin, expressed concern about multiple near mid-air collisions with UAP in the early warning area off Virginia Beach, the same airspace as Graves's encounters, and raised the possibility of shutting down the exercise for safety reasons. Gallaudet reviewed it with his deputy. The next day the email was gone from both their systems. Despite routine in-person meetings of that same group, no one ever discussed it. Gallaudet presumes it was removed in connection with a classified special access program, and has stated publicly that after seeing the report he did not believe the objects represented any known human technology.

The Navy's public position is a study in minimal concession. In September 2019, Joseph Gradisher, official spokesperson for the Deputy Chief of Naval Operations for Information Warfare, told The Black Vault that the Navy's official identifiers for the videos do not match the TTSA names FLIR1, Gimbal and GoFast, the Navy identifies them by the dates of the observations, and then supplied the dates: "[The] dates are 14 November 2004 for 'FLIR1' and 21 January 2015 for both 'Gimbal' and 'GoFast.'" That single sentence established for the first time that GIMBAL and GOFAST were recorded on the same day and may be two aspects of a single event. Gradisher went on: "The Navy has not publicly released characterizations or descriptions, nor released any hypothesis or conclusions, in regard to the objects contained in the referenced videos," while conceding that "The Navy considers the phenomena contained/depicted in those 3 videos as unidentified." Asked for the location, the answer was: "We will not be providing any details on individual reports." The Navy also confirmed the footage had never been cleared for public release when To The Stars Academy published it in December 2017 and March 2018.

Geography can be reconstructed anyway. Filmmaker Dave Beaty worked the deck logs of USS Normandy, the cruiser escorting the Roosevelt, and placed her on 21 January 2015 slightly more than 100 miles east, roughly equidistant between Jacksonville and Savannah, the JAX operating area. A cruiser stays with the carrier during operations, so the deck log fixes the approximate position of the encounter that the Navy declined to disclose.

What happened afterward is the concealment argument's second half. Graves testified that "all UAP videos since 2021 are classified Secret or above, which prevents the American people and even some Members of Congress from seeing UAP videos like the full GIMBAL video recorded by my squadron." The 2021 UAP Task Force preliminary assessment reported eleven near misses with UAP; Graves said he understood the number had grown. In April 2022 the FAA issued an alert to its operations managers that a commercial aircraft over West Virginia had experienced a double attitude and double autopilot failure while flying under a UAP. Graves founded Americans for Safe Aerospace, which by July 2023 had nearly 5,000 members and was working with more than 30 UAP witnesses, most of whom refuse to speak publicly for fear of professional consequences; he also chairs the American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics' UAP Integration and Outreach Committee with roughly 80 volunteer PhDs and aerospace engineers. In his testimony he noted that among the accounts reaching ASA, "in several, sensor data was escalated for analysis and there were profound irregularities in how the data was handled", the same signature as the deleted email, reported independently by other witnesses.

The loop closes in June 2026. Gallaudet, the flag officer whose deleted email is the strongest public evidence that a special access program suppressed a UAP safety warning about this exact airspace, was named to the government's new UAP Science Advisory Council, seated for oceanography, and restricted to unclassified data.

### What it has denied

The Navy's posture is calibrated minimalism: it confirms the dates, confirms the objects are 'unidentified,' declines any characterization, hypothesis or conclusion, and refuses 'any details on individual reports.' It has never publicly explained why a squadron hazard report from W-72 produced no acknowledgement, why an urgent safety-of-flight email to flag officers vanished from SIPRNET within a day, or why the full GIMBAL video remains classified Secret or above eleven years after the encounter.

Roles the board assigns it: concealment, custody, disclosure, oversight.

### Open questions on this node

- Who was the Fleet Forces Command Operations Commander who sent the 2015 'Urgent safety of flight issue' email, and under what authority was it deleted from flag officers' SIPRNET accounts?
- Which special access program does Gallaudet believe was responsible, and has any IG or congressional committee sought the audit logs for that deletion?
- Does the VFA-11 hazard report from the W-72 near-miss survive in Naval Safety Center records, and has it been produced to AARO or NARA?
- How many Range Fouler reports were filed by CVW-1 squadrons during 2014-2015, and where are they held?
- Why has the full unedited GIMBAL video never been released, and under what classification guide is it held Secret or above?
- Do the 2026 PURSUE tranches contain any Roosevelt-era East Coast records, and if not, why were they excluded from a release framed as covering unresolved cases?
- Were GIMBAL and GOFAST recorded by the same aircraft on the same sortie, as the shared date and similar voices suggest, and has the Navy ever confirmed or denied it?

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 478 of 1125, score 31). 8 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D3.

## Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence

Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence is a institution on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2003; axes C2 A3 D2; 7 connections; in the custody band.

In one line: OSD staff element with authority over defence intelligence programs.

### What it is

The Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence and Security, the OSD principal staff element for defense intelligence, counterintelligence, and security policy, chartered by DoD Directive 5143.01 (24 October 2014, incorporating Change 2, 6 April 2020) under 10 U.S.C. 113 and 137 and Executive Orders 12333, 13470 and 13869. It owns DoD security policy, counterintelligence, and the department's interface with the DoD Special Access Program Central Office. Under 50 U.S.C. 3373(b)(3)(B)(i), the Director of AARO reports to the USD(I&S) on all administrative matters of the office.

### Why it is implicated

OUSD(I&S) is the office that produced the paper trail of the Elizondo counter-narrative, and that paper trail is the cleanest documentary evidence in the entire record of a deliberate, dated, signed effort by a defense component to control the public account of a UAP program. Its Director for Defense Intelligence, Garry Reid, wrote a memorandum for record in late December 2017, within a week of the New York Times AATIP story, asserting that Luis Elizondo had 'aggrandized his role' and that to the best of Reid's knowledge Elizondo 'had no job responsibilities related to the AATIP,' while simultaneously initiating an AFOSI inquiry into Elizondo's handling of classified material. The Pentagon then sat on that determination for eighteen months before deploying it publicly through spokesman Christopher Sherwood in June 2019, and afterwards quietly amended the wording from 'no responsibilities' to 'no assigned responsibilities.' The same office holds administrative authority over AARO, the body Congress created to investigate the concealment, and it is the department's SAP security-policy owner. The entity that ran the counter-narrative also holds the keys to the compartments and supervises the investigators.

### The longer account

OUSD(I&S) is where the security state's plumbing and its press strategy meet, and the December 2017 file shows both running at once.

The charter first. DoD Directive 5143.01, reissued 24 October 2014 and updated by Change 2 on 6 April 2020, sets the USD(I&S)'s responsibilities, functions, relationships and authorities pursuant to 10 U.S.C. 113 and 137, 50 U.S.C. 3001 et seq., Public Law 108-458, and Executive Orders 12333, 13470 and 13869. The 'and Security' in the title was added in 2019-2020 and is the whole point: this office does not merely produce intelligence, it writes the rules for who may know things. It coordinates with the Director of the DoD Special Access Program Central Office on SAP matters involving ODNI and the intelligence community, and it runs the department's counterintelligence, law enforcement and security enterprise. Under 50 U.S.C. 3373(b)(3)(B)(i), enacted by Section 6802 of the FY2023 NDAA, the Director of AARO 'shall report... to the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence and Security on all administrative matters of the Office.' Operational and security matters go to the Deputy Secretary and the Principal Deputy DNI; administration, staffing, funding, personnel, facilities, goes to OUSD(I&S). Congress placed the investigative body's housekeeping under the office whose predecessor had spent five years managing the Elizondo problem.

Now the file. Records obtained by The Black Vault under FOIA establish the sequence.

3 October 2017: Elizondo submits a resignation letter, received by his direct supervisor John Garrity III. A second, differently worded resignation letter, the one public since 2018, is later characterized in Reid's memo as having 'uncertain provenance' and as never delivered to the Secretary of Defense.

12 October 2017: Reid learns of the To The Stars Academy launch, at which Elizondo appears as Director of Global Security and Special Programs one week after leaving DoD and states that TTSA plans to provide 'never-before-released footage from real U.S. government systems.' Reid contacts the Air Force Office of Special Investigations. AFOSI finds no 'tangible indicators' of misconduct and declines to open a case.

7 December 2017: The New York Times contacts DoD about AATIP and Elizondo.

15 December 2017: The OUSD(I) Security Officer is notified that Elizondo may have 'misused government systems' and 'may intend to release [U.S. government] footage or information that he obtained during his employment with OUSD(I).'

16 December 2017: 'Glowing Auras and Black Money' publishes, carrying the FLIR1 and Gimbal videos captioned as released by AATIP and courtesy of the Department of Defense. The same two videos appear on TTSA's YouTube channel.

18 December 2017: OUSD(I) discovers a classified email Elizondo sent to a Navy civilian in August 2017 requesting assistance getting videos declassified, with the same videos attached.

21 December 2017: Reid meets AFOSI agents to initiate a formal inquiry into Elizondo's possible mishandling of classified materials. AFOSI concludes the investigation in April 2018. No charges are known to have followed.

23 December 2017: A copy of Reid's memorandum for record carries handwritten marginal notes and initials dated '12/23' believed to be those of Secretary of Defense James N. Mattis, including a question whether Elizondo's 'sudden departure' was a 'counter-intelligence alarm?'

The two propositions in that file cannot both be casually true. If Elizondo had no job responsibilities related to AATIP, the department's own security office had no reason to fear that he would release government footage 'obtained during his employment with OUSD(I),' and Reid had no reason to open a counterintelligence inquiry about material he possessed. The counter-narrative and the counterintelligence action are internally inconsistent, and both were produced by the same office in the same week.

The eighteen-month gap is the other artifact. The determination was reached in December 2017. It was not made public until June 2019, when spokesman Christopher Sherwood told The Intercept that Elizondo 'had no responsibilities with regard to the AATIP program while he worked in OUSDI... up until the time he resigned effective 10/4/2017.' That formulation was later revised to 'no assigned responsibilities', a lawyer's edit, narrowing a claim about what a man did to a claim about what was written in his position description. Departments do not narrow their own denials unless the broader version is indefensible.

Reid himself was removed in April 2022, following a nearly two-year investigation by The Debrief; the stated cause was an inappropriate relationship and favoritism with a colleague, not the Elizondo matter, and sources described him as the person most directly responsible for preventing further UAP video releases and for retaliating against Elizondo. The office continued. Bradley Hansell was confirmed as USD(I&S) by the Senate 61-35 and took office 25 July 2025, succeeding acting Under Secretary Dustin Gard-Weiss.

For an analyst the significance is structural rather than personal. The record shows that when a UAP disclosure event occurred, the response was generated inside OUSD(I&S) by the Director for Defense Intelligence, coordinated with a service investigative agency, briefed to the Secretary of Defense within a week, and held in reserve for eighteen months before public deployment. That is not confusion. That is an information operation run through a security-policy office, and the same office now holds administrative authority over AARO and coordination authority over DoD SAPs.

### What it has denied

OUSD(I&S) is the origin point of the central official denial in the modern UAP record: that Luis Elizondo had no responsibilities, later narrowed to 'no assigned responsibilities', with regard to AATIP. That denial was formed internally in December 2017, withheld from the public for eighteen months, and issued through a departmental spokesperson rather than a signed official. It is contradicted in the same file by the department's own security officers, who feared Elizondo would release footage he had 'obtained during his employment with OUSD(I),' and by Reid's decision to open an AFOSI inquiry into his handling of that material.

Roles the board assigns it: concealment, oversight, custody.

### Open questions on this node

- The exact date, control number and classification caveats of the Reid memorandum for record have not been established from the released copy; reporting places its classification at TS/ORCON but this was not verified against the document.
- Why the December 2017 determination about Elizondo's role was withheld from the public for eighteen months before being issued through a spokesperson in June 2019.
- Who authorized the change in wording from 'no responsibilities' to 'no assigned responsibilities,' and when.
- What the New York Times submitted to DoD on 7 December 2017 that caused the OUSD(I) Security Officer to be notified on 15 December that Elizondo may have misused government systems.
- Whether OUSD(I&S) or its predecessor exercised any authority over AATIP, AAWSAP or any successor effort beyond personnel administration, and whether any such authority is documented in a DoD issuance.
- The identity of the 'Navy civilian' to whom Elizondo's August 2017 classified declassification-assistance email was addressed.
- Whether the colleague who succeeded Reid after his April 2022 removal retained his UAP-related portfolio.
- What administrative controls OUSD(I&S) has exercised over AARO's staffing, funding and access under 50 U.S.C. 3373(b)(3)(B)(i), and whether any of those controls have constrained AARO's historical record work.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 199 of 1125, score 45). 7 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

## DeLonge to Podesta, 'General McCasland', 25 January 2016 (WikiLeaks Podesta Emails 3099)

DeLonge to Podesta, 'General McCasland', 25 January 2016 (WikiLeaks Podesta Emails 3099) is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2016-01-25; axes C2 A2 D1; 6 connections; in the custody band.

In one line: The written record joining DeLonge, Podesta and a General McCasland, released October 2016.

### What it is

An email of 25 January 2016 from Tom DeLonge to John Podesta under the subject line 'General McCasland', published by WikiLeaks in the Podesta Emails on 10 October 2016 as email id 3099, together with the calendar thread of the same days in which the general is himself a correspondent.

### Why it is implicated

It is one of the few documents on this board in which a named general officer is placed, in contemporaneous private correspondence rather than in testimony, inside a conversation about Roswell material and the laboratory at Wright-Patterson. It is a primary record of what was said, not of what is true, and the board carries it on that footing.

### The longer account

The Podesta release is an instrument as much as a document set: raw headers, message ids and reply chains, which is why claims taken off it can be checked rather than believed. This email is the strongest single item in it for this board, because DeLonge disputes the characterisation of the general as a sceptic, says he had been working with him for four months and had given him a four hour presentation, and asserts that the Roswell material was shipped to the laboratory at Wright-Patterson Air Force Base and that the general had been in charge of that laboratory until a couple of years earlier. The calendar thread shows the general was a party to the correspondence and not merely its subject. The corpus opened here adds a second named correspondent inside the same release, read the same way: Bob Fish, writing to Podesta over three days in March 2015, naming the Defense Support Program as a collector of hard data on unidentified flying objects and saying in the same email that he was never fully briefed into that operation directly. The two together are what the release is for on this board: private assertions by identifiable people, dated, with the qualifications the popular retelling drops.

Roles the board assigns it: disclosure, custody.

### Open questions on this node

- Whether any government record corroborates the assertion about Wright-Patterson custody; none opened for this node does.
- The general's own account of the four hour presentation.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 243 of 1125, score 42). 6 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A2/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** DeLonge names the Lockheed leg himself. On 22 February 2016, under the subject 'Advisors', he wrote to Podesta: 'Mr. Weiss from Lockheed SkunkWorks just emailed me asking if there were any updates. I am not expecting much, but if there is anything I can tell him and the General, however small, I would like to respectfully pass it along.' Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email id 15486, https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/15486
- **Tier 1 documented.** DeLonge wrote to Podesta on 25 January 2016 under the subject 'General McCasland': 'When Roswell crashed, they shipped it to the laboratory at Wright Patterson Air Force Base. General McCasland was in charge of that exact laboratory up to a couple years ago.' The raw source gives the date as Mon, 25 Jan 2016 10:04:29 -0800 and the message id as E8428167-CF1B-4CC3-95D2-977A277CC5CB@me.com. Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email id 3099, released 10 October 2016, https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/3099
- **Tier 1 documented.** In the same email DeLonge told Podesta the general was not the sceptic he had been described as: 'I've been working with him for four months. I just got done giving him a four hour presentation on the entire project a few weeks ago', and that 'He not only knows what I'm trying to achieve, he helped assemble my advisory team.' Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email id 3099, https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/3099
- **Tier 1 documented.** Neither Prometheus nor Linda Moulton Howe appears in the release. A search of the WikiLeaks Podesta Emails interface for 'prometheus' returns five emails, all of them American Bar Association technology transfer law circulars addressed to podesta@LAW.GEORGETOWN.EDU, and a search for 'moulton' returns sixteen, none of them hers and none from DeLonge. Thirteen emails in the release were sent from t.delonge@me.com and none of them is among either set. Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, corpus search, https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/?q=prometheus and https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/?q=moulton
- **Tier 1 documented.** The general was a correspondent, not only a subject. The 'DeLonge/Podesta Meeting' thread of 24 and 25 January 2016 runs between Podesta and neilmcc79@gmail.com, who signs 'Neil McC' and whom the Google calendar invitation names as Neil McCasland, copied to t.delonge@me.com, mfisher@hillaryclinton.com, rob.f.weiss@lmco.com and carey.mjd@gmail.com. Podesta organised the meeting as a Google Hangout and moved it to 12:30 Eastern after a blizzard. Source: WikiLeaks, The Podesta Emails, email ids 51979 and 9501, https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/51979 ; https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/emailid/9501
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On Debriefed, episode 82, 3 April 2026, Michael Phillip said the Prometheus and Greek mythology framing reached him through 'the conversations between Podesta and McCaslin and dalong and then by way of dalong lavenda' in the WikiLeaks release, while stating in the same passage that 'I don't know verbatim what he actually said. I'm sort of regurgitating this from talking to lavenda.' The release contains the correspondence he names and does not contain the content he attributes to it. Source: Batch 18 file 01, 0:29:08-0:30:00, research/leads/area52-corpus-18.md; checked against https://wikileaks.org/podesta-emails/?q=prometheus

The link reads: DeLonge's written assertion that the Roswell material went to the Wright-Patterson laboratory. The email asserts to Podesta that 'When Roswell crashed, they shipped it to the laboratory at Wright Patterson Air Force Base' and that the general ran that laboratory until a couple of years earlier. The record establishes that DeLonge asserted this in writing in January 2016, not that it happened. The link reads: Subject of the email and party to the meeting thread, as 'General McCasland' and 'Neil McCasland'. The email names General McCasland and the meeting thread runs through neilmcc79@gmail.com, signing 'Neil McC'. The email gives no rank, service or middle name, so the identification with the board's William Neil McCasland rests on the name and on the node's own Air Force biography, not on the email.
